## User prompt

```text
Goal: replace SQLAlchemy with Tortoise ORM across the Mealie backend.

Fixed decisions:
- Use Tortoise ORM, which is async. When the rewrite is finished, nothing under mealie/
  should import SQLAlchemy.
- Keep support for both SQLite and PostgreSQL.
- Replace Alembic with Aerich. Upgrading existing databases from the old Alembic history
  is out of scope.
- Keep the HTTP API and the Pydantic schemas in mealie/schema unchanged, so the frontend
  does not need to change.
- Database access becomes async. Make routes, services and scheduler tasks async where
  they need to be.
- You may change how the tests set up and access the database (tests/conftest.py,
  tests/fixtures/, and database setup code inside test files). You must not delete tests,
  skip them, mark them xfail, remove or weaken assertions, or change expected values.
- Run the tests with: uv run pytest -n 4 --dist loadfile
  (tests in the same file depend on each other, so they must run grouped by file).
- Do not push, and do not change git remotes or git configuration.

Read AGENTS.md first, especially the "Tortoise ORM rewrite" section. Then do step C5
from that section and nothing else. Follow the conventions and decisions recorded there.
When you finish, run the tests, then update the "Tortoise ORM rewrite" section: record any
new convention or decision, and update the progress table.
```

inspect\nfrom collections.abc import Awaitable, Iterator\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom functools import wraps\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom anyio.from_thread import BlockingPortal\nfrom pydantic import UUID4\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom mealie.repos._utils import NOT_SET, NotSet\nfrom mealie.repos.repository_factory import AllRepositories\n\n_portal: BlockingPortal | None = None\n_portal_loop: asyncio.AbstractEventLoop | None = None\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef use_database_portal(portal: BlockingPortal) -> Iterator[None]:\n    global _portal, _portal_loop\n    _portal = portal\n    _portal_loop = portal.call(asyncio.get_running_loop)\n    try:\n        yield\n    finally:\n        _portal = None\n        _portal_loop = None\n\n\nasync def _await_result[T](awaitable: Awaitable[T]) -> T:\n    return await awaitable\n\n\ndef run_async[T](awaitable: Awaitable[T]) -> T:\n    if _portal is None:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"Async database setup requires the api_client fixture\")\n    return _portal.call(_await_result, awaitable)\n\n\nclass SynchronousRepository:\n    def __init__(self, repository: Any) -> None:\n        self.repository = repository\n\n    def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> Any:\n        value = getattr(self.repository, name)\n        if not callable(value):\n            return value\n\n        @wraps(value)\n        def call(*args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n            result = value(*args, **kwargs)\n            if inspect.isawaitable(result):\n                try:\n SQLAlchemy is\nonly used to compile the existing public query language against the remaining\nrelationship metadata; no synchronous session performs converted database access.\nGroup repositories reuse this compiler until the public relationship/filter\nmetadata moves in the remaining household and recipe conversions.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\nfrom typing import Any\nfrom uuid import UUID\n\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException\nfrom sqlalchemy import Select, nulls_first, nulls_last, select\nfrom sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql, sqlite\nfrom tortoise import connections\n\nfrom mealie.db.models._legacy_users import LongLiveToken, PasswordResetModel, User, UserToRecipe\nfrom mealie.schema.response.pagination import OrderByNullPosition, OrderDirection, PaginationQuery\nfrom mealie.services.query_filter.builder import NonFilterableValueError, QueryFilterBuilder\n\n\nasync def execute(sql: str, values: list[Any] | None = None) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    connection = connections.get(\"default\")\n    values = values or []\n    postgres = connection.capabilities.dialect == \"postgres\"\n    if postgres:\n        parts = sql.split(\"?\")\n        sql = \"\".join(part + (f\"${i + 1}\" if i < len(parts) - 1 else \"\") for i, part in enumerate(parts))\n    values = [\n        (str(value) if postgres else value.hex)\n        if isinstance(value, UUID)\n        else value.astimezone(UTC).replace(tzinfo=None)\n        if isinstance(value, datetime)\n        else value\n        for value in values\n    ]\n    return await connection.execute_query_dict(sql, values)\n\n\nasync def related_record(table: str, key: str, value: Any, **filters: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    # All identifiers are internal constants, never request input.\n    filters = {key: value, **filters}\n    conditions = \" AND \".join(f'\"{name}\" = ?' for name in filters)\n    results = await execute(f'SELECT * FROM \"{table}\" WHERE   "aggregated_output": "\"\"\"Temporary SQL bridges to domains that have not migrated to Tortoise yet.\n\nAll execution uses the current Tortoise connection/transaction. SQLAlchemy is\nonly used to compile the existing public query language against the remaining\nrelationship metadata; no synchronous session performs converted database access.\nGroup repositories reuse this compiler until the public relationship/filter\nmetadata moves in the remaining household and recipe conversions.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\nfrom typing import Any\nfrom uuid import UUID\n\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException\nfrom sqlalchemy import Select, nulls_first, nulls_last, select\nfrom sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql, sqlite\nfrom tortoise import connections\n\nfrom mealie.db.models._legacy_users import LongLiveToken, PasswordResetModel, User, UserToRecipe\nfrom mealie.schema.response.pagination import OrderByNullPosition, OrderDirection, PaginationQuery\nfrom mealie.services.query_filter.builder import NonFilterableValueError, QueryFilterBuilder\n\n\nasync def execute(sql: str, values: list[Any] | None = None) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    connection = connections.get(\"default\")\n    values = values or []\n    postgres = connection.capabilities.dialect == \"postgres\"\n    if postgres:\n        parts = sql.split(\"?\")\n        sql = \"\".join(part + (f\"${i + 1}\" if i < len(parts) - 1 else \"\") for i, part in enumerate(parts))\n    values = [\n        (str(value) if postgres else value.hex)\n        if isinstance(value, UUID)\n        else value.astimezone(UTC).replace(tzinfo=None)\n        if isinstance(value, datetime)\n        else value\n        for value in values\n    ]\n    return await connection.execute_query_dict(sql, values)\n\n\nasync def related_record(table: str, key: str, value: Any, **filters: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    # All identifiers are internal constants, never request input.\n    filters = {key: value, **filters}\n    conditions = \" AND \".join(f'\"{name}\" = ?' for name in filters)\n    results = await execute(f'SELECT * FROM \"{table}\" WHERE {conditions}', list(filters.values()))\n    return results[0] if results else None\n\n\nasync def query_ids(model_name: str, group_id: UUID | None, pagination: PaginationQuery) -> list[Any]:\n    model = {\n        \"User\": User,\n        \"LongLiveToken\": LongLiveToken,\n        \"PasswordResetModel\": PasswordResetModel,\n        \"UserToRecipe\": UserToRecipe,\n    }[model_name]\n    return await query_model_ids(model, group_id, pagination)\n\n\nasync def query_model_ids(model: Any, group_id: UUID | None, pagination: PaginationQuery) -> list[Any]:\n    query: Select = select(model.id)\n    if group_id:\n        # Password reset ownership is derived through its user as well.\n        if model.__name__ == \"ReportEntryModel\":\n            query = query.where(model.report.has(group_id=group_id))\n        elif model is PasswordResetModel:\n            query = query.where(model.user.has(User.group_id == group_id))\n        else:\n            query = query.filter_by(group_id=group_id)\n    if pagination.query_filter:\n        query = QueryFilterBuilder(pagination.query_filter).filter_query(query, model=model)\n    ordering = pagination.order_by or \"created_at\"\n    if ordering != \"random\":\n        for order in ordering.split(\",\"):\n            order = order.strip()\n            try:\n                if \":\" in order:\n                    attribute, direction_value = order.split(\":\")\n                    direction = OrderDirection(direction_value)\n                else:\n                    attribute, direction = order, pagination.order_direction\n                attr = QueryFilterBuilder.get_order_attr(attribute, model, descending=direction is OrderDirection.desc)\n                attr = attr.desc() if direction is OrderDirection.desc else attr.asc()\n                if pagination.order_by_null_position is OrderByNullPosition.first:\n                    attr = nulls_first(attr)\n                elif pagination.order_by_null_position is OrderByNullPosition.last:\n                    attr = nulls_last(attr)\n                query = query.order_by(attr)\n            except NonFilterableValueError as exc:\n                raise HTTPException(\n                    status_code=400,\n                    detail=f'Invalid order_by statement \"{pagination.order_by}\": {exc.message}',\n                ) from exc\n            except ValueError as exc:\n                raise HTTPException(\n/opt/homebrew/bin/uv\n/opt/homebrew/bin/task\n",

## agent_message at 2026-10-11T08:14:00.405055+00:00

The main complication is the C5/C6 boundary: shopping lists and meal plans reference recipes, foods and tools that remain on SQLAlchemy until C6. Their converted repositories will read that data through the active Tortoise transaction, with explicit hydration into the unchanged response schemas.

create them one at a time so the update timestamps are different\n    checked_items: list[ShoppingListItemOut] = []\n    for _ in range(random_int(MAX_CHECKED_ITEMS + 10, MAX_CHECKED_ITEMS + 20)):\n        new_item = list_item_repo.create(\n            ShoppingListItemCreate(note=random_string(), shopping_list_id=shopping_list.id)\n        )\n        new_item.checked = True\n        checked_items.append(list_item_repo.update(new_item.id, new_item))\n\n    # make sure we see all items\n    shopping_list = list_repo.get_one(shopping_list.id)  # type: ignore\n    assert shopping_list\n    assert len(shopping_list.list_items) == len(unchecked_items) + len(checked_items)\n    for item in unchecked_items + checked_items:\n        assert item in shopping_list.list_items\n\n    checked_items.sort(key=lambda x: x.updated_at or datetime.now(UTC), reverse=True)\n    expected_kept_items = unchecked_items + checked_items[:MAX_CHECKED_ITEMS]\n    expected_deleted_items = checked_items[MAX_CHECKED_ITEMS:]\n\n    # store the number of events, so we can compare later\n    params = {\"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe_id}\"}\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, params=params, headers=unique_user.token)\n    response_json = response.json()\n    initial_event_count = len(response_json[\"items\"])\n\n    new_plan = CreatePlanEntry(\n        date=datetime.now(tzlocal()).date(), entry_type=\"dinner\", recipe_id=recipe_id\n    ).model_dump(by_alias=True)\n    new_plan[\"date\"] = datetime.now(tzlocal()).date().isoformat()\n    new_plan[\"recipeId\"] = str(recipe_id)\n\n    response = api_client.post(api_routes.households_mealplans, json=new_plan, headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 201\n\n    # run the task and check to make sure a new event was created from the mealplan\n    run_async(create_mealplan_timeline_events())\n\n    params = {\n        \"page\": \"1\",\n        \"perPage\": \"-1\",\n        \"orderBy\": \"created_at\",\n        \"orderDirection\": \"desc\",\n        \"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe_id}\",\n    }\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, headers=unique_user.token, params=params)\n    response_json = response.json()\n    assert len(response_json[\"items\"]) == initial_event_count + 1\n\n    # make sure the mealplan entry type is in the subject\n    event = response_json[\"items\"][0]\n    assert new_plan[\"entryType\"].lower() in event[\"subject\"].lower()\n\n    # make sure the recipe's last made date was updated\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_slug(recipe_name), headers=unique_user.token)\n    new_recipe_data: dict = response.json()\n    recipe = RecipeSummary.model_validate(new_recipe_data)\n    assert recipe.last_made and recipe.last_made.date() == datetime.now(UTC).date()\n\n    # make sure nothing else was updated\n    for data in [original_recipe_data, new_recipe_data]:\n        data.pop(\"dateUpdated\")\n        data.pop(\"updatedAt\")\n        data.pop(\"lastMade\")\n\n    # instructions ids are generated randomly and aren't consistent between get requests\n    old_instructions: list[dict] = original_recipe_data.pop(\"recipeInstructions\")\n    new_instructions: list[dict] = new_recipe_data.pop(\"recipeInstructions\")\n    assert len(old_instructions) == len(new_instructions)\n\n    for old, new in zip(old_instructions, new_instructions, strict=True):\n        old.pop(\"id\")\n        new.pop(\"id\")\n        assert old == new\n\n    assert original_recipe_data == new_recipe_data\n\n    # make sure the user's last made date was updated\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe_name), headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n    response_json = response.json()\n    assert response_json[\"lastMade\"]\n    assert parse_dt(response_json[\"lastMade\"]).date() == datetime.now(UTC).date()\n\n    # make sure the other user's last made date was not updated\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe_name), headers=h2_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n    response_json = response.json()\n    assert response_json[\"lastMade\"] is None\n\n\ndef test_new_mealplan_event_duplicates(api_client: TestClient, unique_user: TestUser):\n    recipe_name = random_string(length=25)\n    response = api_client.post(api_routes.recipes, json={\"name\": recipe_name}, headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 201\n\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_slug(recipe_name), headers=unique_user.token)\n    recipe = RecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    recipe_id = recipe.id\n\n    # store the number of events, so we can compare later\n    params = {\"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe_id}\"}\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, params=params, headers=unique_user.token)\n    response_json = response.json()\n    initial_event_count = len(response_json[\"items\"])\n\n    new_plan = CreatePlanEntry(\n        date=datetime.now(tzlocal()).date(), entry_type=\"dinner\", recipe_id=recipe_id\n    ).model_dump(by_alias=True)\n    new_plan[\"date\"] = datetime.now(tzlocal()).date().isoformat()\n    new_plan[\"recipeId\"] = str(recipe_id)\n\n    response = api_client.post(api_routes.households_mealplans, json=new_plan, headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 201\n\n    # run the task multiple times and make sure we only create one event\n    for _ in range(3):\n        run_async(create_mealplan_timeline_events())\n\n    params = {\n        \"page\": \"1\",\n        \"perPage\": \"-1\",\n        \"orderBy\": \"created_at\",\n        \"orderDirection\": \"desc\",\n        \"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe_id}\",\n    }\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, headers=unique_user.token, params=params)\n    response_json = response.json()\n    assert len(response_json[\"items\"]) == initial_event_count + 1\n\n\ndef test_new_mealplan_events_with_multiple_recipes(api_client: TestClient, unique_user: TestUser):\n    recipes: list[RecipeSummary] = []\n    for _ in range(3):\n        recipe_name = random_string(length=25)\n        response = api_client.post(api_routes.recipes, json={\"name\": recipe_name}, headers=unique_user.token)\n        assert response.status_code == 201\n\n        response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_slug(recipe_name), headers=unique_user.token)\n        recipes.append(RecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json()))\n\n    # store the number of events, so we can compare later\n    params = {\"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipes[0].id}\"}\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, params=params, headers=unique_user.token)\n    response_json = response.json()\n    initial_event_count = len(response_json[\"items\"])\n\n    # create a few mealplans for each recipe\n    mealplan_count_by_recipe_id: dict[UUID4, int] = {}\n    for recipe in recipes:\n        mealplan_count_by_recipe_id[recipe.id] = 0  # type: ignore\n        for _ in range(random_int(1, 5)):\n            new_plan = CreatePlanEntry(\n                date=datetime.now(tzlocal()).date(), entry_type=\"dinner\", recipe_id=str(recipe.id)\n            ).model_dump(by_alias=True)\n            new_plan[\"date\"] = datetime.now(tzlocal()).date().isoformat()\n            new_plan[\"recipeId\"] = str(recipe.id)\n\n            response = api_client.post(api_routes.households_mealplans, json=new_plan, headers=unique_user.token)\n            assert response.status_code == 201\n            mealplan_count_by_recipe_id[recipe.id] += 1  # type: ignore\n\n    # run the task once and make sure the event counts are correct\n    run_async(create_mealplan_timeline_events())\n\n    for recipe in recipes:\n        target_count = initial_event_count + mealplan_count_by_recipe_id[recipe.id]  # type: ignore\n        params = {\n            \"page\": \"1\",\n            \"perPage\": \"-1\",\n            \"orderBy\": \"created_at\",\n            \"orderDirection\": \"desc\",\n            \"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe.id}\",\n        }\n        response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, headers=unique_user.token, params=params)\n        response_json = response.json()\n        assert len(response_json[\"items\"]) == target_count\n\n    # run the task a few more times and confirm the counts are the same\n    for _ in range(3):\n        run_async(create_mealplan_timeline_events())\n\n    for recipe in recipes:\n        target_count = initial_event_count + mealplan_count_by_recipe_id[recipe.id]  # type: ignore\n        params = {\n            \"page\": \"1\",\n            \"perPage\": \"-1\",\n            \"orderBy\": \"created_at\",\n            \"orderDirection\": \"desc\",\n            \"queryFilter\": f\"recipe_id={recipe.id}\",\n        }\n        response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_timeline_events, headers=unique_user.token, params=params)\n        response_json = response.json()\n        assert len(response_json[\"items\"]) == target_count\n\n\ndef test_preserve_future_made_date(api_client: TestClient, unique_user: TestUser, h2_user: TestUser):\n    recipe_name = random_string(length=25)\n    response = api_client.post(api_routes.recipes, json={\"name\": recipe_name}, headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 201\n\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.recipes_slug(recipe_name), headers=unique_user.token)\n    recipe = RecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    recipe_id = str(recipe.id)\n\n    future_dt = datetime.now(UTC) + timedelta(days=random_int(1, 10))\n    response = api_client.patch(\n        api_routes.recipes_slug_last_made(recipe.slug),\n        json=RecipeLastMade(timestamp=future_dt).model_dump(mode=\"json\"),\n        headers=unique_user.token,\n    )\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n\n    # verify the last made date was updated only on unique_user\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe.slug), headers=unique_user.token)\n    household_recipe = HouseholdRecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    assert household_recipe.last_made == future_dt\n\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe.slug), headers=h2_user.token)\n    household_recipe = HouseholdRecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    assert household_recipe.last_made is None\n\n    new_plan = CreatePlanEntry(\n        date=datetime.now(tzlocal()).date(), entry_type=\"dinner\", recipe_id=recipe_id\n    ).model_dump(by_alias=True)\n    new_plan[\"date\"] = datetime.now(tzlocal()).date().isoformat()\n    new_plan[\"recipeId\"] = str(recipe_id)\n\n    response = api_client.post(api_routes.households_mealplans, json=new_plan, headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 201\n\n    # run the task and make sure the recipe's last made date was not updated for either user\n    run_async(create_mealplan_timeline_events())\n\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe.slug), headers=unique_user.token)\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n    household_recipe = HouseholdRecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    assert household_recipe.last_made == future_dt\n\n    response = api_client.get(api_routes.households_self_recipes_recipe_slug(recipe.slug), headers=h2_user.token)\n    household_recipe = HouseholdRecipeSummary.model_validate(response.json())\n    assert household_recipe.last_made is None\n",

{

  "aggregated_output": "from pydantic import UUID4\nfrom sqlalchemy import select, update\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import joinedload\n\nfrom mealie.db.models.household.shopping_list import ShoppingListItem\nfrom mealie.db.models.recipe.ingredient import IngredientFoodModel, RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel\nfrom mealie.schema.recipe.recipe_ingredient import IngredientFood\n\nfrom .repository_generic import GroupRepositoryGeneric\n\n\nclass RepositoryFood(GroupRepositoryGeneric[IngredientFood, IngredientFoodModel]):\n    def _get_food(self, id: UUID4) -> IngredientFoodModel:\n        stmt = select(self.model).filter_by(**self._filter_builder(**{\"id\": id}))\n        return self.session.execute(stmt).scalars().one()\n\n    def _merge_substitutions(self, from_model: IngredientFoodModel, to_model: IngredientFoodModel) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Moves both directions of the merged-away food's substitutions onto the target.\n\n        Left alone these are cascade-deleted with the food, silently dropping substitutions\n        the user never touched. Substitutions that would become self-referential or duplicate once\n        the two foods are one are deliberately left behind to go with it.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        to_food = to_model.id\n\n        # both sides are read up front, so the sets aren't invalidated as rows are moved\n        existing_substitute_ids = {row.substitute_food_id for row in to_model.substitutions if row.substitute_food_id}\n        existing_source_ids = {row.food_id for row in to_model.substitution_references}\n        outbound = list(from_model.substitutions)\n        inbound = list(from_model.substitution_references)\n\n        for row in outbound:\n            if row.substitute_food_id is not None:\n                # `from -> to` becomes a self-substitution once the foods are one, and\n                # `from -> S` duplicates an existing `to -> S`\n                if row.substitute_food_id == to_food or row.substitute_food_id in existing_substitute_ids:\n                    continue\n\n                existing_substitute_ids.add(row.substitute_food_id)\n\n            # note-only substitutions carry no food reference and always survive the merge\n            to_model.substitutions.append(row)\n\n        for row in inbound:\n            # `to -> from` is the mirror self-substitution, and `X -> from` duplicates an `X -> to`\n            if row.food_id == to_food or row.food_id in existing_source_ids:\n                continue\n\n            existing_source_ids.add(row.food_id)\n            to_model.substitution_references.append(row)\n\n    def _merge_recipe_substitutions(self, from_food: UUID4, to_food: UUID4) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Repoints the recipe-tier substitutions aimed at the merged-away food.\n\n        These rows hang off ingredient lines rather than off the food, so moving the ingredients\n        does not carry them along; left alone they are cascade-deleted with the food, dropping\n        substitutions the recipes still want. Rows that would become self-referential or\n        duplicate once the two foods are one are deleted instead, since that is what they say.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # both ids are matched, so this reads the same whether or not the ingredient move has\n        # been flushed yet: either way those ingredients end up calling for the target\n        merged_food_ids = [from_food, to_food]\n        stmt = (\n            select(RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel)\n            .filter(RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel.substitute_food_id.in_(merged_food_ids))\n            .options(joinedload(RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel.ingredient))\n            .order_by(\n                RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel.ingredient_id,\n                RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel.position,\n            )\n        )\n\n        # the recipe tier has no unique constraint to lean on, so duplicates are collapsed here,\n        # keeping the first the same way the schema does when it prunes a payload\n        repointed_ingredient_ids: set[int] = set()\n        repointed_rows: list[RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel] = []\n        for row in self.session.execute(stmt).unique().scalars().all():\n            if row.ingredient.food_id in merged_food_ids or row.ingredient_id in repointed_ingredient_ids:\n                self.session.delete(row)\n                continue\n\n            repointed_ingredient_ids.add(row.ingredient_id)\n            row.substitute_food_id = to_food\n            repointed_rows.append(row)\n\n        # the session doesn't autoflush, and deleting the food actively loads its substitution rows\n        # to cascade over them, so an unwritten repoint is read back off the database still pointing\n        # at the old food and deleted with it. Only these rows are written: a full flush would also\n        # write the ingredient move, which the delete then undoes by clearing their food.\n        if repointed_rows:\n            self.session.flush(repointed_rows)\n\n    def merge(self, from_food: UUID4, to_food: UUID4) -> IngredientFood | None:\n        from_model = self._get_food(from_food)\n        to_model = self._get_food(to_food)\n\n        to_model.ingredients += from_model.ingredients\n        self._merge_substitutions(from_model, to_model)\n        self._merge_recipe_substitutions(from_food, to_food)\n\n        # Shopping list items reference the food directly rather than through the ingredients\n        # relationship, so they have to be repointed explicitly. Without this the delete below\n        # either violates a foreign key constraint or leaves the item pointing at a missing food.\n        self.session.execute(\n            update(ShoppingListItem).where(ShoppingListItem.food_id == from_food).values(food_id=to_food)\n        )\n\n        try:\n            self.session.delete(from_model)\n            self.session.commit()\n        except Exception as e:\n            self.session.rollback()\n            raise e\n\n        return self.get_one(to_food)\nfrom collections.abc import Iterable\n\nfrom ## agent_message at 2026-10-11T08:21:31.162893+00:00

The full run found one remaining regression pattern: cross-household tests seeded another household’s preferences through the first household’s scoped repository. I’ve corrected those setup calls to use the owning household’s repository; assertions and expected values are unchanged. The other five failures are the documented sandbox socket-binding failures.
